**How to read the numbers in this file.** Every number taken from a generated file sits in a block marked
`<!-- gen:NAME … -->`. `plan-prose.py` renders those blocks from `expectations.json` (generated by `plan-build.py`, indent 1,
never hand-edited), `plan-build.out.txt`, `cells-wave54-final.json` and `landing-manifest.json` (generated by
`land-units.sh --emit-manifest`). `plan-prose.py --check` exits 1 when:
- a block differs from its rendering;
- a hand-written `wave54-final <sec>/<test> <platform> <P|f> <ssim>` citation disagrees with `cells-wave54-final.json`;
- §9 misses a `planDecisions` id;
- §4's landing order breaks a dependency;
- the landing manifest and `expectations.json` disagree on a unit or a revert order.

This is wave-54 lesson 11 made executable: that wave's prose went stale the moment a hand-off regenerated the JSON. A
regeneration is followed by `plan-prose.py --fill` and `--check` in the same commit. Where the prose and
`expectations.json` disagree, the JSON wins and the prose is the defect.

## 0. The contract (rules every lane works under)

Quoted from `docs/BACKLOG.md` ("## Standing constraints", "### Wave 53 lessons", "### Wave 54 lessons"),
`.claude/skills/wave/SKILL.md` and `CLAUDE.md` ("Hard rules for every file"), as they apply here. Everything wave 54's §0 said
still holds (`tools/titan/results/wave54-plan/PLAN.md` §0); the wave-55 deltas are marked **new**.

- **Ring-fence.** "the WPT test `filter-effects/backdrop-filter-basic-blur` belongs to an external session. No test-specific
  code, ever. Generic mechanisms that incidentally move it are reported plainly in the wave PR … never carved out by name."
  - It is in no lane's carriers, no lane's must-not-move list and not in the complement. `plan-build.py` asserts that
    carriers, named lists, `mustNotMoveComplement` and the ring-fence partition the scored cells (`plan-build.out.txt`
    "scored cells 4096 = …"). Mutation M6 in `readers-selfcheck.txt` turns the build red when the ring leaks into the
    complement.
  - **New (D2).** L6 U2 is a generic mechanism: the legacy border band painted behind content. It moves the band of
    basic-blur's opacity-0.6 bordered box inside the opacity group (`android-paint-order.census.out.txt` (2b)).
    - The generator adds that capture to L6 `mayMove` by rule.
    - Both readers apply the ring-fence FIRST: `control-check.mjs` prints `RING-FENCED — report plainly`, and
      `adjudicate.mjs` R5 does not read it.
    - An Android move is reported plainly in the PR with its number. It is never claimed, never reverted for, and never
      named in code.
  - Its cells today: `wave54-final filter-effects/backdrop-filter-basic-blur web P 1`,
    `wave54-final filter-effects/backdrop-filter-basic-blur ios P 0.9526`,
    `wave54-final filter-effects/backdrop-filter-basic-blur android f 0.904`.
- **Honesty labels.**
  - A P→P rise is "picture-correctness". It is claimed ONLY when every key of its `pictureCorrectTally` entry prints
    `→ GEOMETRY OK` / `→ LINEBOX OK` on the closing run (§1).
  - A pass whose picture does not earn it is "DEGENERATE" (`stayDegenerateEvenIfPass`, `degenerateByConstruction`, §1).
    A flip on such a picture is never claimed as a fix.
  - Before any "passes" sentence, open the capture next to the frozen ref: "a score is not a look".
  - A replay never earns a native label; only a device capture plus the family's geometry key does (wave-53 lesson). The
    briefs' replays are SCORE predictions.
  - Say "runtime", never "engine".
- **Single-writer suites.** "Fix/skeptic lanes run focused tests only; the orchestrator runs ONE sequential full sweep
  afterwards — and that sweep INCLUDES the harness suites."
  - **New (wave-54 lesson "UP-TO-DATE in ~1 s").** Every Gradle suite of the sweep runs with `--rerun-tasks`:
    `:converter:test`, `:runtime:testDebugUnitTest` and `:app:testDebugUnitTest`. A sweep line with a ~1 s wall is re-run
    before it is stamped. "Executed" is read from the task outcome, never written by hand.
  - Five lanes compile Compose `:runtime` (L2, L3, L4, L5, L6), and L7 U6-compose adds tests to it.
  - Three lanes compile the SwiftUI package (L1, L4, L5), and L7 U6-swift adds tests to it.
  - Each lane compiles in its OWN worktree (see "Lanes deliver patches" below), so lanes contend for CPU, not for
    files. A lane retries on a lock, never on a foreign file's error.
- **Floors (new rule; wave-54 lesson "A floor on a geometry-gated row is the pass threshold").**
  - Every gating floor is 0.95. A replay magnitude is the row's `to`, never its `floor`; the geometry key is the
    picture-correctness gate.
  - An f→f row carries `floorNA` (C11), so no reader reads a missing floor as 0.
  - `plan-build.py` asserts this; mutation M5 in `readers-selfcheck.txt` (a gating floor set from a replay magnitude) stops
    the build.
  - Any clause a later RESTATE adds is encoded in `expectations.json` and read by `adjudicate.mjs`, or it is not claimed.
    Wave 54 read its must-not-fall clause by hand.
- **Device-probe rule (new form).**
  - Every unit that positions boxes, runs or glyphs on a native is device-probed at stage 1 (`wave55-pre`) with its
    family's geometry key, before any closing gate (wave-53 lesson: a replay cannot predict native row assignment).
  - `expectations.json` `stage1Probe.unitCoverage` asserts that every native unit has a carrier in a stage-1 section.
  - The carriers first measured at stage 2 are listed there by name (§7).
- **Hash-of-record rule.**
  - The installed-build hash of record is read right after `provision rc=0` of the CORPUS launch, with the per-launch wait
    of `hashRead` (round 2, S10: count the `attempt N starting` lines before the launch, wait for a new one, at most 1800 s),
    by `tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/installed-build-hash.sh '<run> corpus launch (…)'`.
  - A fixture-net launch's read is annotated as the race and is never of record. A post-net re-read is taken while the
    devices are still up.
  - Built == installed on one launch is the check.
  - Across launches, the iOS .app digest is a witness: an unchanged-Swift tree reproduces the previous digest
    (`38ea8fdc…` until a Swift unit lands; `../wave55-gate/build-hashes.txt`).
  - **New.** The Android apk sha1 is comparable across launches only when the last fixture-net asset is the same: the apk
    embeds `assets/tmpOutput.json`. This is the wave-55 opening-gate finding, which corrects wave-54 lesson L19
    (`../wave55-gate/_note.md`).
  - **New (round 1, process-holes M2).** Every device PROBE reads the hash too — `stage1Probe.hashRead`, each pre-registered
    re-probe's (`stage1Probe.reprobes`) and `probeRun.hashRead`, rendered in §7 — because the probes decide reverts and
    include or exclude code: without the block a probe is a score, not evidence (BACKLOG). Each block is committed with
    named paths before the next device run; its `HEAD` line is the tree the probe ran, which `ab-arms55.sh` checks for
    `--against` (round 1, S5). With the asset unchanged and no Compose change since `wave55-probe`, the corpus launch's apk
    sha1 equals the probe's: a free second witness beside the iOS digest (round-1 N7).
  - **New (round 2, process-holes S9).** BOTH bundles embed the last test-all IR: `test-all.sh` copies it into the iOS
    `Resources/tmpOutput.json` and the Android `assets/tmpOutput.json` on every fixture, unconditionally (SKIP_IOS does not
    guard it), and `project.yml` bundles `Resources`. So the iOS .app digest is NOT a pure Swift-source witness either: a
    digest or an apk sha1 compares across launches only with the same IR. `installed-build-hash.sh` prints
    `asset sha1 ios=… android=…` (read from the BUILT bundles) in every block, and its "+ uncommitted" reads untracked
    code too (S1).
  - **New (round 2, S10).** The wait is one-shot per LAUNCH: `gate-driver.sh` appends to `driver.log` and provisions at
    every launch (`--resume`, the `--skip-corpus` net launch), so `hashRead` counts the `attempt N starting` lines BEFORE
    the launch and waits (at most 1800 s) for a NEW one; the block is labelled `<run> corpus launch (…, launch k)`.
  - **New (round 2, instrument-teeth S4).** `ab-arms55.sh --against` reads the run's CORPUS-launch blocks (the first names
    the tree), never the last block of the run: a net launch's read is the race by design.
- **Records after restore (new rule).**
  - A device-run script that checks out another tree writes every record (hash blocks, read-outs) only after
    `git checkout -f` back to the branch of record and a verified restore: HEAD, tracked-clean, and the hash file's
    pre-arm sha256.
  - Alternatively, it writes to a path outside the tree and copies the records in afterwards.
  - The wave-55 arm runner is `tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/ab-arms55.sh` (`expectations.json` `abRead.runner`; L7 U3,
    already delivered by the gate-toolkit stage, D19). It writes ALL records once, after the LAST verified restore: a
    per-arm append would sit in the tracked `build-hashes.txt` while the next arm's `checkout -f` runs
    (`../wave55-gate/toolkit.done.md` R-a).
  - Its self-test proves the order: mutations M-L18 and M-per-arm turn it red (`ab-arms55.selftest.out.txt`).
- **Named-path commits while instruments write (new rule).**
  - While any instrument is still writing, orchestrator commits stage NAMED paths only, never a directory-wide `git add`.
  - Bulky intermediates go under the gitignored `tools/titan/runs/<id>/`.
  - Lane evidence of record is `.txt`, or force-added (`*.log` is gitignored). The pointer audit checks `git ls-files` for
    every file a note cites.
- **`--check` and dry-run paths run nothing destructive (new rule).**
  - Every destructive step of a landing, integration, arm or differential script (`git checkout`, `git apply`, `rm`,
    `git branch -D`) sits behind its mode flag.
  - The dry-run path is first exercised in a throwaway worktree (`git worktree add --detach <scratch> HEAD`).
  - No script that rewinds files runs on the shared tree while a lane holds unlanded edits.
  - **This wave's landing script never rewinds at all.** `land-units.sh` routes every mutating git call through one
    guard that exits 70 in `--check` mode, and it never runs checkout, reset, stash, clean or rm on the tree. A unit that
    fails mid-landing is reverse-applied (`git apply --index -R`).
- **Generated, never hand-edited (new rule).**
  - `expectations.json` and `watchlist.txt` are written only by `plan-build.py`.
  - A probe decision enters as an APPENDED row of `probe-decisions/{reverted,restate,contingency}.json` or `holds.json`
    (committed beside the generator, read by default, append-only: `plan-build.py` refuses a regeneration that drops a row
    the installed `expectations.json` records — round 1, S3 / D25); never by editing the JSON. `expectations.json`
    `generatedWith` records the argv and the sha256 of every input, and is the first line of `plan-build.out.txt`.
  - This file's numbers live in `plan-prose.py` blocks ("How to read the numbers", above).
- **One interpreter (C14).** Every probe runs under `/opt/homebrew/bin/python3.12 -B` (`expectations.json`
  `interpreter`). `geometry-gate.py` refuses a cmd off it (exit 2, `readers-selfcheck.txt` B4). `-B` keeps `__pycache__`
  out of the plan directories, and `.gitignore` gained `__pycache__/` at 640276a0.
- **Pins and instruments can fail.** "A new check must be **proven able to fail** (mutation/negative test) before it is
  trusted."
  - Every pin named in §2 has its mutation. The lane EXECUTES it and records red → restore → green with sha256 in its note.
  - Every gating geometry key ships with its fakes (wave-54 lesson "Every gating geometry probe the plan wrote passed some
    skeptic's fake"). `probes-selfcheck.txt` shows each one fails on the pre-fix picture AND (§T) rejects every wrong
    picture of the STANDING SET built from its own fixed picture — target ink removed, a run ±2 / ±3 px, a segment
    shortened, a count-preserving rearrangement — round 1's in `probes-teeth.py`, round 2's in `probes-teeth-r2.py`
    (exit 1 on any accepted fake). **Round 2 (instrument-teeth M6, D31):** the round-1 rules were tuned to round 1's fakes
    and accepted new fakes of the same classes (`plan-skeptic-round-2.instrument-teeth.md` §2), so the claim is bounded:
    a key rejects the fakes in the set, and every new fake a skeptic finds joins it. The set also covers every
    `pictureCorrectTally` key that is not gating (the L3 vrl-rtl report key, the `[K]` controls, the wave-54
    hyphenate-character controls): a tally key that accepts a wrong picture would count it picture-correct. D33's named
    in-tolerance exception is closed by round 4 (D39).
    **Round 3 (verify M2, `fix-r3.done.md`):** a fifth class, ADDED ink — a row painted a second time a few px off, a
    horizontal copy, a doubled glyph, a 2×2 / 3×3 blob of the target's ink in its band — passed the L3 dir-style rows and,
    swept over every lane (`plan-skeptic-round-3.fakes/sweep.py`), 112 faked captures of L1 / L4 / L5 / L6. The probes now
    read how MUCH ink against a same-renderer reference (the base capture translated, or the same picture's twin row), and
    the class joins the set: `probes-teeth-r3.py` (pinned cases) and `probes-selfcheck.txt` §T3 (the full sweep; exit 1 on a
    HOLE). Two faked captures were NAMED residuals (a 2×2 blob touching a face-present 014 glyph); round 4 closed both.
    **Round 4 (verify M1–M4 / S1 / S2, `fix-r4.done.md`, D39):** round 3's claim held for its own variant set only. The
    round-4 verifier's STRICTLY-additive fakes (class ink written only where the destination is not ink: nothing moved or
    erased) passed 17 gating keys inside their own read zones — the ten L4 face-present keys, the five testable L6 `[X]`
    keys, L5 `[H]` ios and `[T]` ios. Each now reads its own zone against a reference picture with two nested ink masks
    (min channel < 200 and the core), XOR ≤ 2: L4 `face xor:` (every band, the Compose no-wrap rows included), L6 `extra`
    with no ring around the base fill, L5 `[H]` / `[T]` twin glyphs, `[C]` / `[I]` and L1 summed over their zones. The
    verifier's generator joins the set: `probes-teeth-r4.py` (every variant it recorded IN-ZONE, pinned) and
    `probes-selfcheck.txt` §T4 (its full sweep over every lane; exit 1 on an IN-ZONE acceptance). Round 3's residuals and
    D33's clip-10 exception are closed. One NAMED residual class stays, the **2-px floor**: a fake adding ≤ 2 px under a
    probe's own mask passes every ±2 / XOR ≤ 2 read by construction (§T4 lists each one); the passing pictures read 0, so
    closing it is a 0-px tolerance, a device question for stage 1. The L4 face reads and the L5 `[T]` read compare a native
    line with ANOTHER rasterizer's pixels (Chromium's ref, Android's tabular '1') until stage 1 re-pins them to the device's
    own decided pixels (§7 stage 1 step 8): a ref tolerance widened to a device face would re-open the class.
  - **Mutations are bracketed (round 2, process-holes S12).** Every pin mutation writes `MUTATING <file> sha256 <pre>` to
    the lane note BEFORE the edit and `RESTORED <file> sha256 <post>` after the restore, so a lane killed mid-mutation
    leaves the sha256 to restore against (§8 step 3).
  - Every new instrument (L7 U2, L7 U3, this file's `plan-prose.py`) is proven red by a fake before it is trusted.
- **No silent fallthroughs.** "If a value variant isn't supported on this platform yet, log it via the PropertyTracker or
  emit a TODO + keep the cross-platform comparison honest." Each brief's breadcrumbs are part of its unit:
  - the `NormalLineBox` log-once;
  - `Sizing[…]` and `ContainingBlock[…]`;
  - the RTL-origin and heading-scope crumbs;
  - the `ownListMarker` TODO.
- **Short, and every line commented.** "target ≤200 lines. If a file grows past ~300, split it."
  - New logic goes in a NEW file of ≤ 200 lines. An oversized file gets only a call site, a defaulted parameter or a
    one-line condition.
  - Files the lanes touch that are already past 300 lines (`wc -l` at HEAD 640276a0):
    - `ComponentRenderer.kt` 8488 and `ComponentRenderer.swift` 6291: seam patches only;
    - `StyleBuilder.swift` 1543: L5 U3-ios, +2 lines;
    - `bidi-bake.test.mjs` 1402: L7 U5, two table entries;
    - `StyleApplier.kt` 1188: L6 U2, three call-site lines;
    - `DynamicValueResolver.kt` 663: L2 U1, 5b333c8a's defaulted parameter and one condition;
    - `BorderSideApplier.kt` 636: L6 U2, a defaulted parameter and two one-line conditions;
    - `SizingApplier.kt` 561: L2 U2, two `else if` call sites and one comment line;
    - `DynamicValueResolverTest.kt` 447: L2 U1's restored rows.
  - Over 200 lines and touched:
    - `ListMarkerRow.kt` 290 and `ListMarkerRow.swift` 230: doc only;
    - `UAElementFontRule.swift` 286: docs;
    - `BorderRadiusApplier.kt` 281: one call site and kdoc;
    - `UAElementFontRuleTest.kt` 243: retargets;
    - `SeamReachabilityTest.kt` 239: module lines;
    - `TypographyAggregate.swift` 238: +1 field.
  - Each lane reports its final deltas. BACKLOG 0(d)'s splits are not done this wave (§9 D15).
- **Verbatim payloads.**
  - Pins use the per-test IR of `tools/titan/runs/wave54-final/sections/*/per-test-ir/`, which is byte-identical to
    `wave55-open`'s (`../wave55-gate/control-open.txt`: wire 1435/1435).
  - PNGs are read from the same run and from the frozen refs
    (`tools/wpt/refs/9b5435e55e0b54a6cd09c1c563861eb3c999cef1/white-black-ink-font-lh-imgpad-htmlpins-rootbg-uamargin/`).
- **No edits under a running gate.** "Nothing a running gate executes or builds from is edited … while a gate (or a device
  A/B) runs on this host." Lanes pause whenever the orchestrator runs a device step.
- **Never kill a foreign process.** T5 (`start_adb_detached` kills the adb server at every provision) stays in
  Known-broken.
- **A pre-registered lost list: EMPTY.** "A lost cell outside the list pre-registered before the closing gate stops the
  ship until its cause is found in the picture AND the wire document."
  - `expectations.json` `expected.lost` is `[]`: no lane changes an instrument on the gate's scoring path.
  - L7 U2 and U3 are not executed by the gate. U4 changes comments and a console string. U5 is predicted wire-identical
    and lands only behind [W-L7a].
- **Seams are never edited by a lane.**
  - The seam files:
    - `runtimes/compose/src/main/java/com/styleconverter/runtime/core/renderer/ComponentRenderer.kt`
    - `runtimes/swiftui/Sources/StyleConverterRuntime/Renderer/ComponentRenderer.swift`
    - `apps/web-harness/src/sdui/ComponentRenderer.tsx`
    - `tools/titan/extract-fixture.mjs`
  - A lane that needs one delivers `tools/titan/results/wave55-<lane>/seam-<n>.patch`, one patch per revert unit that
    needs a hunk. The patch is cut on the tree it lands on (a cut on 0d0d3a3d is fine where no earlier patch touched its
    context), and its header names the base tree, the seam file's sha256 at base and the content anchor.
  - This wave has hunks in the first two files only (§3).
- **Lanes deliver patches; they never edit the shared tree (new model, D18).**
  - Each lane works in its own throwaway worktree off HEAD, named on its note's `TREES:` line, under disjoint ownership
    (`briefs-audit.md` §1: 0 paths owned by two lanes). It never commits.
  - **Worktree names (round 2, process-holes S4 / S5).** Every lane, skeptic, fix, S1 and [W-L7a] worktree is created as
    `git worktree add --detach <scratchpad>/wave55-<role>-<lane> HEAD` — never as a Workflow-isolated worktree
    (`wf_<run>-<n>`): `beforeEveryDeviceRun` finds the trees whose Gradle daemons it stops by their `/wave55-` path, and
    `kill_own_gradle_daemons` spares a daemon that served ANY unlisted root. Every Swift build in such a worktree passes
    `-derivedDataPath <wt>/.dd` (Catalyst DerivedData is keyed per checkout path, about 4 GB each), and §8 step 9½ removes
    the worktrees and their DerivedData.
  - **Provisioning (round 1, process-holes S10)**, in the lane's worktree before its first build:
    `cp <shared>/apps/android-harness/local.properties <wt>/apps/android-harness/` (gitignored: without it every Compose
    suite fails with "SDK location not found"), `ln -s <shared>/node_modules <wt>/node_modules` (and the workspace links
    it holds), and the `TREES: <wt>` line written FIRST, before any build. A lane that "fixes" provisioning on the shared
    tree breaks D18.
  - **Revert states compile (round 1, S12).** Before delivery the lane compiles, and runs its focused tests on, every state
    its `revertOrder` chains can produce — every chain of `lanes.*.revertUnits[].revertOrder` (and of
    `contingencyUnits[].revertOrder` once landed), on its own worktree — and records it in the note
    (`revertStatesCompiled:`). The S1 skeptic re-runs it
    on the landed tree. A revert state that does not build would fail at provision and cost a device window.
  - In its lane dir it delivers `unit-<U>.patch` (its owned files) and `seam-<n>.patch` (one seam file plus its
    patch-borne test files) per revert unit, together with `_note.md`, which has one section per unit. This is
    `landing-manifest.json` `deliveryContract`, rendered in §4.
  - The orchestrator lands them with `tools/titan/results/wave55-plan/land-units.sh`: one commit per unit, in §4 order.
    It refuses a touched path that is dirty at HEAD, so a lane edit on the shared tree is caught, never committed and
    never wiped.
  - Two lanes may touch one path only in `sharedPaths`: the two seam files and `SeamReachabilityTest.kt`.
  - Every device read on the landed tree uses the UNION carrier set (C1).
- **Skeptic census.** "A builder lane's blast radius is re-derived by a skeptic with its OWN census before it is believed."
  - A lane's carrier set is its BEHAVIOURAL census (wave-54 lesson "The static carrier predicate under-counted
    CBB-android by three captures").
  - A census that grows regenerates the plan before landing. D2 was this wave's first such regeneration, made at
    generation.
- **No scratchpad pointers.**
  - Every artifact goes under `tools/titan/results/wave55-<lane>/` as soon as it exists.
  - Each lane note ends in `STATUS: COMPLETE` or `STATUS: PARTIAL — <what is left>` and carries a `TREES:` line (written
    first). `TREES:` lines name the lane's throwaway worktree by design and are exempt from §8 step 18's scratchpad grep
    (round-1 N6).
  - **New (round 1, process-holes M3).** `land-units.sh --check` refuses a note that is not `STATUS: COMPLETE`, a note with
    no `TREES:` line, and any lane-dir patch the note does not name on a `patch: <name> sha256 <64 hex>` line (written after
    the final restore) or names with another sha256, plus any `*.patch` the ORDER table does not know. A limit-killed lane
    never lands; it is resumed (§8 step 3) and re-cut first (`land-units.selftest.r1-EF.out.txt` E0–E12).
  - Each note has one section per revert unit naming exactly that unit's paths. Every unit commit's body points at it,
    and the landing verifies that each commit's staged set equals the paths its patches name.
- **Device windows only through the orchestrator.**
  - Builder lanes run no Chromium, simulator or emulator step, no `gate-driver.sh`, no `test-all.sh`.
  - **New (round 1, S9; round 2, S1 / S4 / S5).** Every device run starts with `beforeEveryDeviceRun` (§7), an executable
    string run from the shared tree's root. Its first clause refuses a tree whose code differs from HEAD — tracked, staged
    or UNTRACKED (`git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` over runtimes, apps, converter, tools/titan outside
    results, and the root `Package.swift` the iOS harness builds; under D18 a stray lane write is most likely a NEW file).
    Its second refuses under 20 GiB free. It then stops the Gradle daemons of the shared tree, of every `TREES:` line and
    of every registered `/wave55-` worktree.
  - They write the exact command, its expected output and the decision it feeds under "## ORCHESTRATOR WINDOW REQUESTS".
  - This wave's windows:
    - stage 1 and stage 2 (every native unit);
    - [W-L7a] (Chromium plus converter);
    - the L4 probe-time arm and the closing arms;
    - L6 [W1].

  one way.
- Seam regions are disjoint (§3).
- The revert units of `landing-manifest.json` `counts` (+ its contingency), each one commit (§4).

### L1 · hyphen-ios-linebox

Brief `hyphen-ios-linebox.md`. Queue: obligation 0(f), re-trued by the brief (§2 "Verdict on 0(f)'s wording"): U3b is not
the iOS defect, it removed a compensation; the iOS TEXT RUN's `line-height: normal` box is wrong. **One unit (NB-ios), one
SwiftUI seam hunk, and a pre-registered contingency (NB-ios-b).**

- **Targets.**
  - `wave54-final css-text/hyphens/hyphenate-character-001 ios P 0.9611`
  - `wave54-final css-text/hyphens/hyphenate-character-003 ios P 0.957`
  - `wave54-final css-text/hyphens/hyphenate-character-004 ios f 0.9477`: the wave-54 prediction miss that the drop-U3b
    arm attributed to U3b.
  - The DEGENERATE `wave54-final css-text/hyphens/hyphenate-character-002 ios P 0.9534`: TextKit emergency breaks.
- **What the pictures show** (brief §1, `hyphen-ios-linebox.look-001…004.png`).
  - The strings and breaks are the ref's.
  - Every group from the second on sits one to two rows HIGH.
  - In the drop-U3b arm the group firsts sit on the ref, but lines 4–5 of a 4–5-line group sit one row high: that is the
    iOS run's own pitch.
- **Mechanism** (brief §2).
  - **iOS today.** A run whose `line-height` is the declared keyword `normal` lays out on the face's RAW metrics: 18.625
    px/line for DejaVu Sans Mono 16 px ((1901 + 483) / 2048 × 16). The box is unpinned, and SwiftUI ceils the N-line box:
    94 / 75 / 56 px for 5 / 4 / 3 lines.
  - **Chromium and Compose.** Chromium (the frozen refs) and Compose lay `normal` on the ROUNDED metrics:
    round(14.85) + round(3.77) + round(0) = 19 px, so their boxes are 95 / 76 / 57.
  - **Why it surfaced now.** The old 20-px `<br>` cancelled the one-row shortfall at every group start. U3b's 19.2 px
    removes 0.8 px of that cancellation per group, which renders as −1 / −2 / −2 rows.
  - **Evidence for round-each.** It rests on the Arial 92 ref box (105 px, where round-sum would give 106) and on Compose
    agreeing on three faces (`hyphen-ios-linebox.facemetrics.out.txt`). The Blink source itself is cited from memory
    (brief §9 R3).
- **Change (NB-ios).**
  - NEW `NormalLineBox.swift`: pure statics, ≤ 200 lines.
    - `blinkLineSpacing` rounds each of ascender, descender and lineGap half away from zero (lroundf).
    - `faceMetrics` resolves the rendered face in exactly `LineBoxMetrics.contentHeight`'s order.
    - `usedLineHeight(effective:declaredNormal:lineCountIsExact:wptCapture:horizontalWritingMode:…)` returns `effective`
      verbatim whenever it is non-nil; Blink's box iff `declaredNormal && wptCapture && lineCountIsExact &&
      horizontalWritingMode`; otherwise nil.
    - **Round 1 (D22, generator-truth M1).** The `horizontalWritingMode` guard is new. Without it NB-ios would MOVE the
      stage-1 byte-identity control `hyphens-vertical-001 ios`: its space-less `'hyphen\xadation'` box (`__2-320`,
      `VERTICAL_RL`, Width 3em) reads as ONE line to the whitespace proxy (`singleLineText`, `lineCountIsExact`,
      `renderedLineCount` 1), while TextKit draws three. The pin would clamp it to one 19-px box. The brief's §4 amendment
      has the evidence and the census line now says "declined by the NB-ios horizontal guard".
    - A Blink box below the raw line height logs once through `PropertyTracker.logOnce`, because `.lineSpacing` cannot be
      negative.
  - Seam 1 hands that L to the existing wave-30 pin (CSS 2.1 §10.8: N line boxes of exactly L):
    - cut the `leading` statement (:5055-5069);
    - insert `let usedLineHeight = …(…, horizontalWritingMode: horizontalWritingMode)` after `lineCountIsExact`
      (:5151-5152), passing PlaceholderLabel's own stored flag (:4868, set at :3220);
    - re-insert `leading`, reading it;
    - `pinnedBoxHeight(lineHeightPx: usedLineHeight)` (:5154).
  - That is one call site, one argument and one moved statement: no new logic in the oversized file.
- **Spec.**
  - CSS 2.1 §10.8.1: `normal` is UA-defined, "a 'reasonable' value based on the font"; every line box starts with a strut.
  - CSS 2.1 §10.6.3: an auto-height block is as tall as its line boxes.
  - CSS 2.1 §15.8: `font: 16px monospace` resets `line-height` to `normal`.
- **Contingency NB-ios-b** (brief §4 and §9 R1).
  - Trigger: the three gating rows print `LINEBOX WRONG` with the reason `anchor: group 1 line 1 offset 1` and nothing
    else. That is the snapping model under which SwiftUI ceils the 15.04-px baseline.
  - Change: seam 1b (:5335) gives a pinned declared-normal run a top pad of 0 and a bottom pad of 2× the half-leading.
  - It lands through an appended `probe-decisions/contingency.json` row and a regeneration (NB-ios's revertOrder becomes
    [NB-ios-b, NB-ios]; the `drop-NB-ios` arm then drops NB-ios-b first), and a pre-registered css-text re-probe (§7, D13).
- **Cross-wave revert order.** NB-ios is ADDITIVE on the wave-54 U3b: once NB-ios lands, U3b is load-bearing on iOS (replay
  Rd: -004 ios goes P → f). Revert NB-ios before U3b, never U3b alone (`revertRule` 6).
- **Pins** (`NormalLineBoxTests`, Catalyst; brief §4 table):

  | pin | asserts | mutation(s) that must turn it red |
  |---|---|---|
  | P1 | the 19-px anchor | — (anchor) |
  | P2 | Arial 92 → 105, DejaVu 20 → 24 | M1 round the sum; M2 ceil the sum |
  | P3 | lroundf | M3 `.toNearestOrEven` |
  | P4 | the three guards | M4, M5 |
  | P5 | identity for every declared or calibrated run | M6 |
  | P6 | face resolution does not drift from `LineBoxMetrics.contentHeight` | M7 |
  | P7 | the verbatim -001 host's StyleBuilder inputs | — (input pin) |
  | P8 | D22: hyphens-vertical-001 `__2-320`'s properties, verbatim from the wave54-final per-test IR, give `nil`; the same call with `horizontalWritingMode: true` gives the Blink box | M8 drop the guard |

  The Catalyst run also LOGS `lineCountIsExact` and `renderedLineCount` for the five SAME controls, into the note. The census
  asserts those values; it does not derive them. all-prop-001's `'321'` in a 0.5em box is the other space-less run.

  Catalyst cannot see the defect (its `UIFont` metrics are integral), so the seam has no failing unit test. Stage 1 and
  the geometry key are its check.
- **May run.**
  - `xcodebuild test -scheme StyleConverterRuntime -destination 'platform=macOS,variant=Mac Catalyst,arch=arm64'` with
    `-only-testing:` for `NormalLineBoxTests`, `LineHeightNormalTests`, `LineBoxPinTests`, `WPTCaptureModeTests` and
    `LineHeightWireTests`.
  - They run in the lane's own worktree, with seam 1 applied there; the patches are cut from that tree (D18).
- **ORCHESTRATOR WINDOW REQUESTS:** none of its own. Its one-section probe (brief §8) is stage 1 (css-text). Its A/B arm
  `drop-NB-ios` runs at the closing gate (§8).
- **Risks** (brief §9).
  - R1, the snapping rule (LOW-MED). NB-ios-b is the answer. The in-radius controls are the tripwire: four after C5 moved
    disclosure-styles ios to L5.
  - R2, a thin margin over the arm values: Rm beats the arm by +0.0002 / −0.0001 / +0.0001 / +0.0008.
  - R5, sequencing (above).
- **Not staffed:** U3b-exact (§5).

Round 1 (plan-skeptic instrument-teeth; D24) re-ruled the gating keys against plausible WRONG pictures built from their own fixed picture; the standing fakes live in `probes-teeth.py` and are read through the real gate in `probes-selfcheck.txt` §T. Round 2 (D31) re-ruled them again against new fakes of the same four classes, now in `probes-teeth-r2.py` (the `teeth` line below names each new reason).
- M7 added the `glyphs:` reason: NB-ios moves line boxes, never glyphs, so every line's ink crop must equal the same line of
  the BASE capture (`--base`, wave55-open), translated by whole rows only. x-shifted text, one shifted line, a missing
  hyphenate-character and two swapped lines are WRONG; the cmd now takes `--base <base>`.

Brief `cbb-android.md`. Queue: obligation 0(g), the wave-54 probe's one revert. **Two units, landed and reverted as a pair
(U1 never without U2, U2 never without U1). Android only, one Compose seam hunk (the old one, re-cut). GO-SMALL.**

- **Targets.**
  - `wave54-final filter-effects/backdrop-filter-nested-border-radius-clip-3 android f 0.9574`: the 0(g) target. U2 needs
    U1.

    HEAD + the old unit (brief "Evidence").
    - `wave54-final filter-effects/backdrop-filter-nested-border-radius-clip android P 0.9649`
    - `wave54-final filter-effects/backdrop-filter-nested-border-radius-clip-2 android P 0.9689`
    - `wave54-final filter-effects/backdrop-filter-nested-border-radius-clip-4 android P 0.977`
    - `wave54-final css-flexbox/abspos/abspos-autopos-htb-ltr android P 0.9966` (red-ink DEGENERATE), and the -vlr-ltr /
      -vrl-ltr twins.
- **The record's hypothesis, corrected** (brief §2, `cbb-android.look.out.txt`). It is right about the size and wrong about
  the cause.
  - The missing 20 px on each axis is #middle's padding: 10 + 10.
  - U1 fixed the containing-block CHANNEL, rightly: the out-of-flow `#backdrop-filter` is sized 200×100.
  - The IN-FLOW percentage frame stays wrong. Under WPT capture, `SizingApplier.inflateForContentBox` passes a PERCENT size
    through untouched, and `fillMaxWidth/Height(fraction)` makes the percentage the BORDER box.
  - So #middle (`width/height: 100%; padding: 10px`) is drawn as a 200×100 border box, its content box is 180×80, and
    #inner's `overflow: hidden` clips the correctly sized green to 180×80.
  - On the probe this showed as green (36,36,215,115) against the ref's (36,36,235,135).
- **U1: CBB-android re-landed (5b333c8a).**
  - `ContainingBlockBands.subtracts(properties, wptCaptureMode)`:
    - under WPT capture, an effective content-box publishes its declared size;
    - a declared border-box still subtracts;
    - the dark stage always subtracts, and breadcrumbs `ContainingBlock[dark-stage-content-box-bands]` on an explicit
      content-box.
  - Spec: CSS 2.1 §10.1 / §10.2, css-ui-3 §3.1.
  - The two existing files are byte-identical to 5b333c8a's parent, so its hunks apply as committed.
  - The test's census path moves to `wave54-final`.
  - **C8** adds the breadcrumb-assertion row; its mutation deletes the `markUnhandled`.
- **U2: PercentContentBoxBand (new).**
  - `owns(raw, cfg, bandPx, clampOwnsAxis)` is true iff all of these hold:
    - WPT capture;
    - content-box;
    - a `Relative(PERCENT)` size;
    - a non-zero band;
    - PercentSizeClamp does not own the axis.
  - `targetPx = round(maxPx × fraction + bandPx)`.
  - The `(child, reported)` pair comes from `ExactWidthOverflow.measureSpec`: the box lays out at its size and reports the
    incoming max, so the parent does not grow.
  - Placement offset 0. An unbounded axis is identity.
  - Two one-line `else if` call sites in `applySizing`, after :123 and after :132.
  - Breadcrumbs: `Sizing[dark-stage-percent-content-box-band]`, `Sizing[percent-clamp-content-box-band]`.
  - Spec: css-ui-3 §3.1; CSS 2.1 §10.2 / §10.5; css-overflow-3 §3 (the container's clip, #outer's `overflow: hidden`,
    hides the overflow).
- **C13.** The chain-order argument (sizing → #middle's own overflow clip → padding, inside StyleApplier's modifier chain) is
  cited by SYMBOL in L2's pins and note, never by line: L6 U2 adds lines to `StyleApplier.kt` above the cited region, and
  L6 owns that file.

    measure-scope direction.
- **U2: the physical-left origin for an RTL parent.**
  - It applies to an RTL parent with a px Width whose child resolves an inline inset.
  - An outer layout modifier places the zero-flow anchor at `x = −constraints.maxWidth`, so the ink lands at
    `colWidth − maxWidth + left = left`. A `right`-only child lands at `colWidth − w − right`.
  - An auto-width RTL parent, or a child with both inline insets `auto`, keeps today's placement with a breadcrumb. There
    are 0 such corpus carriers: all 11 RTL Column-loop parents declare a px Width, and all 13 of their children declare
    `left`.
  - Spec: CSS 2.1 §10.3.7 (`left` is physical), css-position-3.

### L4 · ua-heading-margin

Brief `ua-heading-margin.md`. Queue: obligation 0(h), and the wave-54 L5 held face it gates. **Four units: U1-android and
U1-ios (the margin), U2-android and U2-ios (the held face, restored behind U1). GO by default (D1); `--go-small` holds
U2-android.**

- **Targets.**

  | cell | iOS | Android |
  |---|---|---|
  | inset-005 | `wave54-final css-text-decor/text-decoration-inset-005 ios f 0.8995` | `wave54-final css-text-decor/text-decoration-inset-005 android f 0.9` |
  | inset-006 | `wave54-final css-text-decor/text-decoration-inset-006 ios f 0.8987` | `wave54-final css-text-decor/text-decoration-inset-006 android f 0.8993` |
  | inset-014 | `wave54-final css-text-decor/text-decoration-inset-014 ios f 0.9238` | `wave54-final css-text-decor/text-decoration-inset-014 android f 0.923` |
  | inset-011 (margin only) | `wave54-final css-text-decor/text-decoration-inset-011 ios f 0.6999` | `wave54-final css-text-decor/text-decoration-inset-011 android f 0.6852` |
- **Mechanism** (brief §2 A–B, traced at HEAD; this closes 0(h)'s "Not traced" clause).
  - Each folded `<h1>` is `position: absolute` inside a relative `<div>`, with no author margin.
  - Chromium's UA sheet gives it the 2em bold face and `margin-block: .67em` of that face: 21.44 px, or 17.42 px for 014's
    26-px monospace face.
  - Neither native gives a NESTED out-of-flow box any UA margin. The in-flow collapse plan bails the whole container on any
    out-of-flow child, and the wave-52 T6 rule covers canvas-hoisted roots only.
  - So the heading paints about 21 px high, at the 16-px regular face.
- **U1 (`UaPositionedHeadingMargin` / `UAPositionedHeadingMargin`, NEW in `spacing/`).** One more tag-keyed UA cascade
  step, where `ListStyleUaRule` and `UAElementFontRule` already run.
  - It fires iff ALL of these hold:
    - WPT capture is on;
    - the tag is h1–h6;
    - the element's OWN post-reset list declares `Position ABSOLUTE | FIXED` (css-position-3 §2.1);
    - at least one block edge is undeclared (author beats UA per edge, css-cascade-4 §6.1);
    - the writing mode is horizontal (a vertical one is logged once).
  - It appends `MarginTop` / `MarginBottom {px: uaBlockMarginEm(tag) × F}`, where F is the UA-CASCADE font size.
  - F is independent of the face gate, so U1 and U2 are separate units.
  - It returns the SAME list instance when it does not fire.
  - Headings only this wave. A non-heading out-of-flow UA-margin tag is logged once (R9, queued).
- **U2 (the held face).**
  - `UAHeadingFoldGate.{kt,swift}` is restored from `tools/titan/results/wave54-ua-heading-face/held/` (`SHA256SUMS.txt`
    12/12).
  - A heading with children stands the face down iff its runs do not fold: 005 / 006 / 014 are `Folded`, 011 is `Bailed`.
  - Seam 3 replaces `held/seam-1.foldgate.patch`, which no longer applies at HEAD.
  - Seam 4 is regenerated from `held/seam-2.foldgate.patch` (which applies) on top of seam 2.
- **Why GO with two DEGENERATE-labelled Android passes (D1).**
  - iOS wraps an abspos 2em heading at the containing block: `wave54-final css-text-decor/text-decoration-inset-009 ios P 0.9585`.
  - Compose measures an abspos child of a relative parent UNBOUNDED:
    `wave54-final css-text-decor/text-decoration-inset-009 android P 0.9638`, one line running to x373.
  - So 005 / 006 android pass with "fox" clipped. They are on `stayDegenerateEvenIfPass` and `degenerateByConstruction`,
    and never count as gains.
  - 014 android, with a definite `16ch`, is faithful.
- **C4.**
  - The wave-54 `ua-heading-face.geometry.py` inset-011 native CONTROL keys are dropped. They are superseded by
    `[M] text-decoration-inset-011 ios|android` (gating): U1 moves 011 by 21 px by design.
  - The wave-54 must-not-move rows of the eight inset natives are not carried: they are this lane's carriers.
- **C10 / C12.**
  - The module lines for `UaPositionedHeadingMargin.kt` (seam 1) and `UAHeadingFoldGate.kt` (seam 3, directly under it)
    ride patch-borne, CUT ON HEAD at the pinned anchor after `"content/PseudoGeneratedBox.kt",` (:159) (round 1, M1; §3).
  - The lane proves both revert orders (seam 3 / seam 1 and seam 4 / seam 2) in a `git archive` export tree.
- **Pins** (brief "Pins").
  - U1, both natives:

    | input | expected | mutation(s) that must turn it red |
    |---|---|---|
    | 005 / 006 / 011 `<h1>` | 21.44 | the painted basis (10.72) |

- **May run.**

### L5 · counter-suffix-rows

Brief `counter-suffix-rows.md`. Queue: obligations 0(j) (counter-suffix's out-of-family rows) and 0(n) (stale comments: five
sites, not two). **Seven units: U1-ios, U2o-ios, U2i-ios, U2o-android, U2i-android, U3-ios, D.** No web unit, no wire
change.

- **Targets.**
  - `wave54-final css-counter-styles/counter-suffix ios P 0.9873`: DEGENERATE on rows 3–6 and 9.
  - `wave54-final css-counter-styles/counter-suffix android P 0.989`: DEGENERATE on rows 5–6.
  - The inside `、` gap: `wave54-final css-counter-styles/cjk-decimal/css3-counter-styles-005 ios P 0.993` and
    `wave54-final css-counter-styles/cjk-decimal/css3-counter-styles-005 android P 0.9759`.
  - `wave54-final css-counter-styles/counter-style-at-rule/disclosure-styles ios f 0.6127`: reached by U1, identity
    predicted.
  - Control: `wave54-final css-counter-styles/counter-suffix web P 1`.
- **Mechanisms** (brief §2).
  - **A. iOS draws the Hebrew suffix before the letter** (rows 3–4, `.א foo`).
    - SwiftUI resolves the marker `Text`'s paragraph direction from the first strong character (UAX #9 P2/P3), and `א` is
      strong R.
    - css-lists-3 §3.1.1 makes `::marker` an isolate whose base direction is the ITEM's `direction` (css-writing-modes-4
      §2.2), here ltr.
    - 2 markers in 1 document disagree.
  - **B. Both natives keep the CJK marker 4 px left** (rows 5–6).
    - The 4 px is `ListMarkerRow.gapPt` / `gapDp`: the stand-in for the suffix's trailing space, which the counter bake
      trims (`tools/titan/counter-style-table.mjs:19-27`).
    - `cjk-decimal`'s suffix `"、"` has no trailing space (css-counter-styles-3 §6.1).
    - The same gap sits between the 34 INSIDE `、` markers of css3-counter-styles-001 / -004 / -005 and their text.
  - **C. SwiftUI ignores `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` on a text run.** `FontVariantNumericApplier.swift` is an
    identity, so row 9's `1` is proportional. 6 components, 1 document.
- **Units** (brief §4).
  - **U1-ios `ListMarkerBidi`.**
    - `painted(s, itemIsRTL)` returns `s` UNCHANGED when its natural direction equals the item's (the isolate is then the
      identity), and `LRI + s + PDI` / `RLI + s + PDI` otherwise.
    - So 215 of 217 baked markers, and every native-table marker under an ltr item, stay byte-identical by construction.
    - Seam 1: the marker branch's call, `markerPlacement`, and three `Text` strings.
  - **U2o / U2i `ListMarkerSuffixGap`** (byte-parallel twins).
    - 0 when the painted marker's last scalar is U+3001, the stand-in 4 otherwise. The key is the STRING, not the type.
    - U2o is the outside hang: seam 2 on iOS (:4618), seam 4 on Compose (:4482).
    - U2i is the inside HStack / Row: seam 3 on iOS (:4646), seam 5 on Compose (:4665).
  - **U3-ios.**
    - `FontVariantNumericApplier.contribute` sets `agg.tabularNums`.
    - `StyleBuilder` mirrors it into `TextConfig.tabularNums`.
    - Seam 6 composes `.monospacedDigit()` on the label font.
    - Declared-only, not inherited.
  - **D (comment-only).**
    - `ListMarkerOutsideHang.swift`: the re-cut of `wave54-rtl-marker-bake/hunk-for-orchestrator-1.patch`, with its false
      sentence corrected. Also the `.kt` twin.
    - `KoreanHangulFormal.swift`, `ListMarkerText.swift`, `ListMarkerStyle.swift`.
    - **C7:** `tools/titan/counter-style-table.mjs:21-23`, the SUFFIX NOTE ("a U+3001 suffix has no stand-in gap"). It is
      true only after U2o. It is on the extract path, but a comment changes no wire byte.
- **Spec.**
  - css-lists-3 §3.1.1 / §3.4 / §3.5;
  - css-writing-modes-4 §2.2;
  - UAX #9 P2 / P3 / N1 / N2;
  - css-counter-styles-3 §3.4 / §6.1 / §6.2 / §6.4 / §7.1.1 / §7.1.3;
  - css-fonts-4 `font-variant-numeric`.
- **D5.** `css3-counter-styles-005 android` is predicted to FALL by 0.0018 (replay 0.9759 → 0.9741). It is horizontally
  right, but Android's 2–4 px vertical offset and lighter CJK face dominate the SSIM. The row is `down-preregistered`, so
  rule 2 fires only at Δ ≤ −0.0038.
- **C4.**
  - The wave-54 `rtl-marker-bake [M] counter-suffix` and `lists-bakes counter-suffix` keys are registered under L5 as
    CONTROLS.
  - Their `rows 0-207 identical` clause is dropped on iOS / Android (rows 3–6 / 5–6 move by design; `[K]` replaces it) and
    kept on web.
  - Their verdicts on L5's `ok-all` pixels were EXECUTED, not derived: all six stay OK (`probes-selfcheck.txt` §C4).
- **C5.** `disclosure-styles` ios is L5's U1-ios carrier, an identity row ("read the picture if it moves"). Its Android
  capture is L6 may-move and is not in L5's identity read.
- **C12.** The lane dry-runs reverting U1-ios with U2i-ios applied (the :4646 / :4650 neighbourhood) in an export tree,
  before stage 1.
- **Pins** (brief §4).

  | unit | pins | mutation(s) that must turn it red |
  |---|---|---|
  | U1 | pure + Catalyst raster | `painted` returns its input; always wrap (identity pins go red) |
  | U2 | `("一、") == 0` … `("") == 4` | drop the U+3001 test |
  | U3 | applier and StyleBuilder | restore the identity |
  | D | the grep + `git diff --stat` comment-only | — |
- **May run** (the plan's list; the brief names its test files, not a command):
  - Catalyst `-only-testing:` for `ListMarkerBidiTests`, `ListMarkerSuffixGapTests`, `FontVariantNumericTabularTests` and
    the existing `ListMarker*` suites;
  - `:runtime:testDebugUnitTest --tests '*ListMarker*'`.
- **ORCHESTRATOR WINDOW REQUESTS:** none of its own; [W1] is stage 1 (css-counter-styles). The U1 fallback (re-cut

  - R3: `Font.monospacedDigit()` on the bundled `Font.custom("Inter")` (MED). `[T]` decides; a `UIFontDescriptor`

### L6 · android-paint-order

Brief `android-paint-order.md`. Queue: obligation 0(k), the two residues the wave-54 second reader found under passing
cells. **Three units: U1 PO-selfpaint-android, U2 PO-sides-android, U3 FO-pin (test only). Compose only, no seam.**

- **Targets.**
  - `wave54-final filter-effects/backdrop-filter-clip-rect android P 0.9559`: 416 px of the red menu borders UNDER the
    navbar's blue border.
  - `wave54-final css-flexbox/abspos/flex-abspos-staticpos-align-self-safe-001 android P 0.9619` and
    `wave54-final css-flexbox/abspos/flex-abspos-staticpos-align-self-safe-002 android P 0.9635`: up to 1152 px of a black
    container border across a yellow / teal child. Plus four grid gating pictures.
  - `wave54-final css-masking/clip-path/clip-path-filter-order android P 0.9548`: 0(k)(2), the order question.
- **Mechanism** (brief §2 A).
  - Compose paints every border band AFTER the element's content. androidx foundation 1.11.4 `BorderModifierNode` starts
    every outline branch with `drawContent()` (javap of the AAR), and `BorderSideApplier`'s slow path is
    `drawWithContent { drawContent(); paintSide… }`.
  - The children are composed inside that node, so the parent's band overpaints them. CSS 2.1 Appendix E paints them over
    it.
- **U1.** `applySelfPaint` installs `behindContentBorder(width, color, shape)` in place of androidx `border(…)` at
  `BorderRadiusApplier.kt:207`. NEW `BehindContentBorder.kt` paints the band THEN `drawContent()`, with the androidx band
  arithmetic copied: rect / simple-RoundRect / path on the same Outline.
- **U2.** The legacy route's band moves inner of the background and paints behind content.
  - `StyleApplier`'s legacy branch keeps radius-clip + outline outer, keeps ColorApplier, then calls
    `BordersFacade.applySidesBehindContent`.
  - `BorderSideApplier.applyBorders(…, behindContent = true)`: the fast path is U1's painter on `RectangleShape`; the slow
    path is the four `paintSide` calls before `drawContent()`, through `drawInOrder`.
  - The default `false` keeps every other caller byte-identical.
- **U3.** A JVM pin on the verbatim clip-path-filter-order wire: the clip is installed OUTER of the drop-shadow `DrawBehind`
  (filter → clip; filter-effects-1 §5, css-masking-1). It closes 0(k)(2) at the chain level. Whether the shadow RENDERS on
  device is [W1]'s question.
- **Spec.**
  - CSS 2.1 Appendix E. E.2: a box's border is painted before its in-flow and positioned descendants; outlines last.
  - filter-effects-1 §5.
  - css-masking-1.
  - css-color-4 §3.3: an opacity group.
- **D2 and the ring-fence.** U2 moves the band of `backdrop-filter-basic-blur`'s opacity-0.6 bordered box inside its
  opacity group (census (2b)). That box is outside the 252-document scan because it has no child. `mayMove` gains it by
  rule, and it is reported plainly (§0).
- **C2.** 12 stems of the raw 252 are L2 / L3 carriers (`sharedStems`).
  - They are subtracted from `mayMove`.
  - A move there is the carrier lane's, attributed by the closing `drop-L6-U2` arm.
  - It is never an L6 revert by itself (`revertRule` 6x).
- **Pins** (brief §4).

  | pin | asserts | mutation(s) that must turn it red |
  |---|---|---|
  | P1 | `drawInOrder`'s order and the installed element | swap the calls; restore `result.border(` |
  | P2 | the band geometry | drop the `ceil`; drop the `max(0, …)` clamp |
  | P3 | the folded chain on the verbatim `.flex` wire and the DOTTED `background-clip-002` wire | revert the StyleApplier hunk (both go red) |
  | P4 (U3) | the clip is outer of the shadow | move `applyClipPath` below; drop the shadow branch |
- **May run** (the plan's list; the brief names its test files, not a command):
  `:runtime:testDebugUnitTest --tests '*BehindContentBorder*' --tests '*BorderRadiusSelfPaint*' --tests '*BorderSidesBehindContent*' --tests '*EffectsClipFilterOrder*' --tests '*BorderSide*' --tests '*BordersFacade*'`.
- **ORCHESTRATOR WINDOW REQUESTS:**
  - none before stage 1; its [W2] lane probe is stage 1 (filter-effects, css-masking, css-flexbox, css-grid);
  - the closing arm `drop-L6-U2`;
  - [W1], evidence only, after the closing gate (D11).
- **Risks** (brief §9).
  - R1, raster parity (the decisive one). A behind-content band that does not rasterise exactly like androidx's changes
    bands everywhere. The defence: the 10 `[ID]` rows, the fixture net and the control. The 9 gate fixtures hold 9
    bordered components (`android-paint-order.gate-fixtures-scan.out.txt`).
  - R2, U2's reach. 77 documents carry a bordered component with own text. An unpredicted mover stops the ship until its
    picture shows a band now under content. Dropping U2 is the fallback; U1 and U3 stand alone.
  - R5, the slow path is device-unexercised: raster identity only.

**Ordering constraints and their resolutions.**
- **Cross-lane.**
  - In `.kt`, hunks of different lanes are ≥ 785 lines apart.
  - In `.swift`, L4 (:340-356), L5 (:3973-:4650), L1 (:5055-5160) and L5 seam 6 (:5537) do not touch.
  - So the cross-lane order is free, with one exception: L1 seam 1 lands before L5 seam 6, which is re-anchored by
    content, because L1's cut-and-reinsert moves the lines after :5055.
  - Every patch is cut against 0d0d3a3d and applied after `git apply --check`. When the check fails on the tree a patch
    lands on, its lane RE-CUTS it (a fix-lane task); it is never hand-merged.
- **Intra-lane (C12).**
  - L4 applies seam 1 → 3 and seam 2 → 4, and reverts 3 → 1 and 4 → 2. Both are proved in a `git archive` export tree.
  - L5 lands U1-ios → U2o-ios → U2i-ios and U2o-android → U2i-android, and reverts U2i before U2o.
  - L5 also dry-runs reverting U1-ios with U2i-ios applied: seam 3 sits 4 lines above seam 1's :4650 hunk.
- **C13.** L2 cites StyleApplier's chain by symbol, so L6 U2's lines (an owned file, not a seam) can land before it (§4
  puts them first).
- **No hidden source pins** (`seam-registry.md` §1 / §2). No existing test pins renderer text inside a wave-55 region,
  except L4's own `UAElementFontRuleSeamWiringTest.kt`.

**Totals.**
- The registered seam patches are the rows of the two tables above (`.kt` and `.swift`), plus the contingency 1b; the
  machine list is `landing-manifest.json` `units[].seams`.
- No separate `integrate-seams.sh` this wave (D17). `land-units.sh --check` is that check:
  - it runs `git apply --check` on each patch against the current tree alone;
  - it then runs `git apply --check` on the whole series at once, cumulatively, in §4 order;
  - it runs every unit's revert order reverse-applied on the fully landed series (C12), in an EXPORT TREE outside the
    repo.
- The focused suites of the touched module are re-run by the lane on its own worktree, with its patches applied, before
  delivery.

## 4. Landing order and the unit commit list

1. **Commit the plan** with named paths: this directory's untracked files and the wave-55 gate files in
   `tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/`, including the toolkit's `ab-arms55.*` (L7 U3 is optional, D19).
2. **Builders, skeptics and fix lanes** (§8). Each lane delivers its `unit-<U>.patch` / `seam-<n>.patch` files and
   `_note.md` in its lane dir (§0, D18).
3. **[W-L7a]**, before the landing, on throwaway worktrees (§2 L7, D12). If it holds, U5 lands at its place; otherwise
   `--hold L7:U5`.
4. **The landing:** `tools/titan/results/wave55-plan/land-units.sh`, written at planning by the landing stage (D17).
   - First `land-units.sh --check`, in a throwaway worktree (`--root <scratch>`), then on the shared tree. It is
     read-only, and every mutating call exits 70 in that mode.
   - Then `land-units.sh` on the idle host. It refuses (exit 2, nothing touched) while a gate, probe or arm runs, on a
     staged index, on a dirty touched path, or on a lane file written in the last 120 s.
   - It lands a records commit first: the seven lane dirs, plus L4's six gitignored wave-54 dry-run `out/` files
     force-added (obligation 0(o)). That way every unit commit holds code paths only and reverts alone.
   - Then one commit per unit, in the order below, each unit's patches applied as one set right before its commit.
   - A failure mid-unit reverse-applies that unit and stops (exit 3); resume with `--from <L:U>`.
   - The list below is rendered from `land-units.sh`'s GENERATED manifest. `plan-prose.py --check` re-checks it,
     independently, against `expectations.json` (every unit exactly once, the same revert orders) and against the
     dependencies listed under it.

**`deliveryContract`** (what each lane delivers in its lane dir; `land-units.sh --check` refuses the rest):
- `unitPatch`: unit-<U>.patch: the unit's owned files; absent only for a seam-only unit
- `seamPatch`: seam-<n>.patch: one hunk set in exactly ONE seam file (the registered one) + its patch-borne test files
- `cutOn`: the tree it lands on (records + every earlier unit); a patch cut on 0d0d3a3d is fine wherever no earlier patch touched its context. SeamReachabilityTest.kt (round 1, M1): cut on HEAD at the writer's PINNED anchor — L4 seams 1 → 3 after "content/PseudoGeneratedBox.kt", (:159), L5 seam 4 after "images/ImageCandidateChain.kt", (:167), L3 seam 1 → unit-U2 after "transforms/TransformInheritance.kt", (:182); never at the list end
- `note`: _note.md (round 1, M3): `TREES: <worktree path>` written FIRST (before any build); one section per unit naming exactly its paths; one line `patch: <name> sha256 <64 hex>` per delivered patch, written after the final restore; `revertStatesCompiled:` — every state of the unit's revertOrder chains compiled and its focused tests run on the lane's worktree (round 1, S12); ends `STATUS: COMPLETE` (anything else is refused by --check)
- `forbidden`: a unit patch touching a seam file; a seam patch touching another seam file; a patch touching a lane records dir; two lanes touching one path outside sharedPaths; a unit patch and its own seam patch touching one path; renames and copies

**`sharedPaths`** (the only paths two lanes may both touch): `runtimes/compose/src/main/java/com/styleconverter/runtime/core/renderer/ComponentRenderer.kt`, `runtimes/swiftui/Sources/StyleConverterRuntime/Renderer/ComponentRenderer.swift`, `runtimes/compose/src/test/java/com/styleconverter/runtime/core/renderer/SeamReachabilityTest.kt`.

**Why this order.** `plan-prose.py --check` re-asserts the constraints marked ✓ independently of `land-units.sh`, which
asserts every one of its own (`landing-manifest.json` `orderConstraints`; round-2 N8: the count is that file's, never typed).
- ✓ **Within each lane, `revertOrder` reversed.** A unit that must be reverted BEFORE X lands AFTER X: L2 U1 → U2; L3 U1 →
  U2; L4 U1-<p> → U2-<p>; L5 U2o → U2i; L6 U1 → U2. L2 is one group: U1 is committed only after U2 is checked on top of
  it.
- ✓ **L7's `landingOrder`**, with L7 first, as in wave 54 (tooling first).
  - U2 and U3 are the instruments later windows use.
  - The U6 pins pin today's behaviour of files no wave-55 lane edits.
  - U4 is comments and one console string.
  - U5, the only extract-route change, lands only behind [W-L7a].
- ✓ **L4 → L3 → L5** (C10: one `SeamReachabilityTest` list with three writers; with the pinned anchors of round 1 the
  order is the review order, not a necessity — F1 lands them in §4 order, E5(c) of the process-holes repro in reverse).
- ✓ **L1 NB-ios before L5 U3-ios** (seam 6 is re-anchored after L1's seam 1). The manifest puts NB-ios after L5's other
  `.swift` seams, which is `seam-registry.md` §6's top-to-bottom sequence.
- ✓ **L5 D after U2o on both natives** (C7).
- **L6 before L2** (C13). Asserted by `land-units.sh`: L2's pins and note are finalised on the tree that has L6's
  StyleApplier lines.
- **L4 before L2**, `ComponentRenderer.kt`'s top-to-bottom order (`seam-registry.md` §6). Asserted by `land-units.sh`.
  Cross-lane hunks are ≥ 785 lines apart, so this is a convention, not a necessity.

5. **One sequential full sweep** (wave skill Phase 4; §0 single-writer, `--rerun-tasks` on every Gradle suite).
   - Suites:
     - converter;
     - web runtime;
     - compose `:runtime:testDebugUnitTest --rerun-tasks`;
     - android-harness `:app:testDebugUnitTest --rerun-tasks`;
     - swiftui (Catalyst);
     - `npm -w apps/web-harness run test`;
     - tooling (`node --test tools/visual/*.test.mjs tools/titan/*.test.mjs`), now including `wire-differential.test.mjs`
       and `label-chrome-check.test.mjs`;
     - IR conformance (`node schema/conformance/run.mjs --emit`);
     - the ios-harness XCTest, in a device-idle window.
   - Every lane moves a count. Restamp README, CLAUDE.md, STATUS and the tree READMEs, then run
     `tools/visual/doc-staleness-check.sh` → exit 0.
   - No coverage-audit count moves: no lane adds a `<Name>Applier` file (each brief's docs section).

**Revert order on the landed tree** (rule 6).
- Within a lane, latest first.
- L1 NB-ios before the wave-54 U3b; never U3b alone.
- L2 U2 and U1 together.
- L3 U2 before U1.
- L4 U2-<p> before U1-<p>.
- L5 D before U2o (round 1, S1 / D27: D's SUFFIX NOTE is true only while U2o is on the tree), then U2i before U2o.
- L6 U2 before U1.
- A combined-tree skeptic fix commit on a landed unit (`-S1`) lands one per unit and reverts before its unit (wave-54 §10
  item 6 ¶4).

## 6. The score of record, and which JSON each reader reads

Written BEFORE any lane lands. The wave-55 gate readers are already in `tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/`. The generator
wrote them, `readerChanges` lists their deltas from the wave-54 copies, and they are calibrated (`readers-calibration.txt`;
`controlCalibrations` / `adjudicateCalibrations`). Same host as wave 54, so every platform's capture is byte-deterministic
run to run (wave-54 lesson; `wave55-open` reproduced 1435/1435 on all three).

<!-- gen:record (plan-prose.py; do not edit by hand) -->
- `scoreOfRecord`: `node tools/titan/score-gate.mjs wave55-open wave55-final --watch tools/titan/results/wave55-plan/watchlist.txt --movers 0 --json tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/score-final.json > tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/score-final.movers0.txt`
- `geometryGate.closingCmd`: `/opt/homebrew/bin/python3.12 -B tools/titan/results/wave55-plan/geometry-gate.py wave55-final --base wave55-open --json tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/geometry-final.json > tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/geometry-final.out.txt`
- `adjudicate`: `node tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/adjudicate.mjs tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/score-final.json --geometry tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/geometry-final.json`
- `scoreReadout`: `node tools/titan/score-gate.mjs wave55-open wave55-final --watch tools/titan/results/wave55-plan/watchlist.txt --movers 0.005 --json tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/score-final.movers0005.json > tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/score-final.txt   (the human-readable mover list for the PR; adjudicates nothing)`
- `corpusSnapshotSource`: tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/score-final.movers0005.json (the <score.movers0005.json> of tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/make-snapshot55.mjs — D20, round-1 S4: the wave-55 snapshot generator, refusing ORDER / NOT THE 0.005 RECORD / NO CELL REVIEW / NO INSTALLED BUILD; its perCellDiff.movers counts |Δ| >= 0.005 as its scorer string says — score-final.json, the --movers 0 record, would publish ≈4000)
- `geometryGate.what`: runs every lane's geometryProbes (one pinned interpreter: `interpreter`), prints one PASS/FAIL per expect key, names the unit each failing gating row reverts (rule 4), exits 1 on a gating FAIL or a probe self-check failure, 2 on a STALE json, 3 when no gating key FAILs but one is UNMEASURED; with --sections a key whose keyCells section is not listed prints NOT PROBED and gates nothing (C15). R6 = exit 0 with no --sections. A CONTROL FAIL never changes the exit; controls that sit on CARRIERS (L1's 3 hyphenate-character ios, L5's 2 rtl-marker-bake / lists-bakes natives, L4's ua-heading-face block-in-inline / web rows on non-carriers excepted) are read in the cell review and named in the PR

| file (`closingGateFiles`) | written by · read by · never read by |
|---|---|
| `tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/score-final.json` | scoreOfRecord (--movers 0). Read by adjudicate.mjs (R1-R5, R6 read-out) and by nothing else; adjudicate.mjs refuses every other record: exit 2 ORDER (prev is not wave55-open) or NOT A --movers 0 RECORD |
| `tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/score-final.movers0005.json` | scoreReadout (--movers 0.005). Read by tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/make-snapshot55.mjs (corpusSnapshotSource) and the PR mover list ONLY; never adjudicated (adjudicate.mjs exits 2 on it; make-snapshot55.mjs exits 2 NOT THE 0.005 RECORD on the --movers 0 one) |
| `tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/geometry-final.json` | geometryGate.closingCmd. R6 is that command's EXIT; adjudicate.mjs --geometry reads its rows only to retire a degenerateByConstruction gain, and refuses it (exit 2 GEOMETRY PAIR) unless run/base equal the score record's cur/prev |

**`readerChanges`** (the wave-55 copies of the two gate readers):
- `control-check.mjs`: tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/control-check.mjs = the wave-54 copy with the expectations path → tools/titan/results/wave55-plan/expectations.json and two classes: a changed capture of ringFenced.captures is "RING-FENCED — report plainly" (read FIRST: basic-blur android is also in L6 mayMove, D2), one in unionMayMoveCaptures[<platform>] (or lanes.<id>.mayMove with --lane) is "may-move" (not a leak: its score is bound by adjudicate R5 through mustNotMoveComplement)
- `adjudicate.mjs`: tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/adjudicate.mjs = the wave-54 copy with the expectations path → tools/titan/results/wave55-plan/expectations.json, --base default wave55-open, R5 also walking mustNotMoveComplement.cells (labelled "complement"), and (round 2, generator-truth S2) R6 never retiring a cell stayDegenerateEvenIfPass lists (inset-005 / -006 android stay DEGENERATE whatever their [F] row prints)
- `roundTwo`: control-check.mjs refuses (exit 2) when a CARRIER capture is present on one side only (instrument-teeth S3: a carrier may change, never vanish); every reader refuses a flag given without its value (exit 2, S5); geometry-gate.py refuses --base equal to the run outside --self-test and a --base run without sections/ (S2)
- `calibrated`: tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/readers-calibration.sh → tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/readers-calibration.txt (K1-K14 control-check, J1-J6 adjudicate; each new branch is shown load-bearing by a mutated copy run in a throwaway worktree)

**`controlCalibrations`**:
- wave54-final -> wave54-final (= wave54-final -> wave55-open: the opening gate measured them byte-identical) → **HOLDS** (tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/readers-calibration.txt K1)
- wave54-open -> wave54-final → **FAILS with leaks: wave 54's landed changes are not wave-55 carriers (the wave-55 carrier set can fail)** (K2)
- both pairs, --lane L7-instruments (an EMPTY carrier set) → **HOLDS on the identity pair, FAILS on the wave-54 pair** (K3)
- wave54-final -> a synthetic two-section post run (one pixel nudged on a may-move, the ring-fenced and a complement android capture) → **exactly 1 LEAK (the complement); may-move and RING-FENCED printed, not leaks; without the complement nudge it HOLDS; a copy without the may-move class FAILS it** (K4-K6)

**`adjudicateCalibrations`**:
- the identity record relabelled wave55-open → wave55-final → **FAIL R4: the 9 gating flip rows missed (nothing landed)** (tools/titan/results/wave55-gate/readers-calibration.txt J1)
- all-met synthetic (the 9 gating f→P rows at P 0.97) → **R1-R5 hold, exit 0** (J2)
- all-met + one complement cell +0.003 → **FAIL R5, labelled "complement"; a copy without the complement read does not see it (exit 0)** (J3, J5)
- a record whose prev is not the base; a --movers 0.005 record relabelled → **exit 2 ORDER; exit 2 NOT A --movers 0 RECORD** (J4, J4b)
<!-- /gen:record -->

**Why `--movers 0`** (wave 54 fix round 1, M1).
- `score-gate.mjs` lists a same-verdict cell only when |Δ| ≥ the threshold, and `adjudicate.mjs` reads a cell absent from
  every list as "did not move".
- With `--movers 0` every scored cell is in a list. So adjudicate's floor read sees every gating cell, and its
  must-not-move read applies the 0.002 rule to every named and complement cell.
- Several wave-55 rows are predicted to move by less than 0.005: the boundary-shaping ×8 within 0.0001, the grid `[X]`
  rows ±0.0005, and -005 android −0.0018.

**Rules** (R1–R9 are the plan's table, as in wave 54).

| rule | expectation | on failure |
|---|---|---|
| **R1 lost** | `expected.lost` = ∅ against `wave55-open`. | Stops the ship until the cause is found in the picture AND the wire document; the unit is reverted or fixed, then its sections re-probed. Never ledgered, never excused by a re-run. |
| **R2 denominator** | unmeasured-now = 0 and newly measured = 0. | An unregistered instrument change: find it before reading any gain. |
| **R3 completeness** | 30 sections, every column 48/48/48 (css-cascade 43/43/43). | The exit-7 recipe; never `SKIP_<P>`. |
| **R4 capture control** | `control-check.mjs wave55-open wave55-final`: the union carriers (§2 "Per-lane counts and the union") minus every carrier a probe decision withdrew; L6's may-move class printed, not a leak; the ring-fence printed RING-FENCED and reported plainly. Every other composed capture is decoded-pixel identical. Per-lane counts are printed. | A leak is a finding: bisect by commit (revert rules 5, 6). |
| **R4b wire control** | Every `per-test-ir/*.json` byte-identical to `wave55-open`: no wire carrier, no renumbering shadow (`wireRenumbering.expectedShadow` `{}`). | A changed document is an extraction or converter leak: stop. |
| **R5 gains** | Every gained cell is a carrier with a prediction row. Every gain is PNG-checked against the frozen ref before it is written as a pass. A gain on `degenerateByConstruction` is DEGENERATE unless adjudicate's R6 RETIRES it on `degenerateRetirementCheck` (iOS only this wave). | A gain outside the list is looked at and explained in the PR, never claimed unseen. |
| **R6 picture-correctness** | `geometryGate.closingCmd` exits 0: every gating key of a unit still on the tree PASS, and no probe self-check failure. A gating key UNMEASURED → exit 3, not a pass. WITHDRAWN keys never gate. Every report key is read and labelled. Controls are expected PASS; a control FAIL on a carrier is read in the cell review and named in the PR (`geometryGate.what`). A tally entry counts only when all of its keys are OK. | A target whose key is not PASS keeps its old label (DEGENERATE as before): said, not counted. |
| **R7 must-not-move** | adjudicate's R5 over the `--movers 0` record. It reads every lane's named `mustNotMove` cell (the list-entry and distinct-cell counts are `readers-calibration.txt` J5's "R5 must-not-move cells" and `plan-build.out.txt`'s "named must-not-move") and every `mustNotMoveComplement` cell (labelled "complement"), each within 0.002 and never lost or gained. L6's may-move cells are must-not-move cells (of the complement or of a lane's named list), so their 0.002 band IS this rule (round-1 N1). | A move is an R4 leak or a carrier mislabel: diagnose it. |
| **R8 fixture net** | `BASELINE=1 ./test-all.sh --gate-set` exits 0 on all 9 gate fixtures against committed baselines, all-then-color included. This is where L6's raster parity meets the 9 bordered components of the gate fixtures. | The exit-code recipes (wave skill Phase 5). |
| **R9 tooling on the ship tree** | The full tooling suite is green on the tree that ships, with L7 U2's and U6-tooling's new tests. | Fixed before the ship, never ledgered. |

**adjudicate.mjs's own numbering** (its header):
- its R1–R3 are R1–R3 above;
- its R4 is the gating-floor read: every gating row at 0.95 (count in `plan-build.out.txt`);
- its R5 is R7 above, with the complement;
- its R6 is R5's DEGENERATE retirement, a read-out that never changes the exit.

R6 of the table above is `geometry-gate.py`'s exit.

**Revert rule 2 is a probe rule** (wave-54 R3-N5). At the closing gate, `adjudicate.mjs` enforces the floors and
must-not-move only. A directed non-gating row that falls between `wave55-probe` and `wave55-final` should not happen on
this byte-deterministic host; if it does, the cell review names it in the PR.

(Round 2, process-holes S11: the two caveats this section carried were deleted — both became false at the round-1
regeneration. The snapshot's reader is `wave55-gate/make-snapshot55.mjs` (D20, D28), and `control-check.mjs` COUNTS a
capture absent before or after as a leak, and refuses the read when the absent one is a carrier (`readerChanges`).)

**Expected totals:** §1 "Expected movement".

**`beforeEveryDeviceRun`**: `export PROJECT_ROOT=/Users/dranak/Documents/Projects/Style-Converter/.claude/worktrees/trusting-bohr-bd6fbf; [[ -z "$(git --no-optional-locks -C "$PROJECT_ROOT" status --porcelain --untracked-files=all -- runtimes apps converter tools/titan Package.swift ':(exclude)tools/titan/results' ':(exclude)*.md')" ]] || { echo "the tree differs from HEAD in code (tracked, staged or untracked): a device run would build it (round-1 S9, round-2 S1)"; exit 2; }; (( $(df -g "$PROJECT_ROOT" | awk 'NR==2{print $4}') >= 20 )) || { echo "disk < 20 GiB free (round-2 S5)"; exit 2; }; source "$PROJECT_ROOT/tools/titan/own-processes.sh" && kill_own_gradle_daemons "$PROJECT_ROOT" $(sed -n 's/^TREES: *\([^ ]*\).*/\1/p' "$PROJECT_ROOT"/tools/titan/results/wave55-*/_note.md 2>/dev/null) $(git --no-optional-locks -C "$PROJECT_ROOT" worktree list --porcelain | sed -n 's/^worktree //p' | grep -E '/wave55-')`

- `beforeEveryDeviceRunNote`: Run the string verbatim with bash from any directory — e.g. bash -c "$(jq -r .beforeEveryDeviceRun /Users/dranak/Documents/Projects/Style-Converter/.claude/worktrees/trusting-bohr-bd6fbf/tools/titan/results/wave55-plan/expectations.json)" — exit 2 = refused (code differs from HEAD, or under 20 GiB free), exit 0 = both checks hold and the Gradle daemons of the shared tree, of every TREES: line and of every registered /wave55- worktree are stopped. Never ./gradlew --stop: the /wave55- filter keeps other sessions' worktrees out, and kill_own_gradle_daemons leaves (and names on stderr) any daemon that also served an unlisted checkout, so no foreign process is stopped. Bash only (it uses [[ ]] and (( )); not sh).
